| 240 | 2026-03-16 | 1.08c — The "running machine" aesthetic payoff | 1 run | Aesthetics (Wave 1 competitive analysis feeding aesthetics). Breadth-first: aesthetics at 22 files (tied for least-explored). Five design options for sealed watch aesthetic payoff: A "The Signal Symphony" (signal lines as primary aesthetic — colored dashed lines pulsing across the board like a circuit board lit at night; per-channel audio tones creating emergent harmonic chord; strongest GIF-able/spectator option but risks visual clutter at 6+ channels), B "The Heartbeat Board" (context bars as primary aesthetic — vertical thermometers per unit pulsing with arrivals/evictions; vital-signs-monitor metaphor; less clutter but doesn't show inter-unit connections), C "The Dual Layer" (zoom-gated: signal web at full zoom, context bars at close zoom; best of both worlds but breaks sealed watch "no tools" purity if zoom is allowed), D "The Emergent Choreography" (unit movement ghost trails as primary aesthetic — long-exposure photography of autonomous behavior; most accessible/TikTok-optimized but hides the information architecture), E "The Factory Floor" (production queue as rhythmic anchor — factory conveyor animation + unit materialization as heartbeat; Factorio's core aesthetic). Recommended "Layered Symphony": signal lines primary + context bars secondary + ghost trails tertiary + factory rhythm as anchor, with Inspector-only zoom detail. Aesthetic ramp: M1-2 ghost trails only → M3 signal lines appear → M4 context bars visible → M5 factory rhythm begins → M6-10 all layers active. Five-component anatomy of "running machine" satisfaction: rhythmic predictability, spatial legibility, emergent complexity, proportional aesthetic response, pride-of-ownership freeze frame. Research: SpaceChem waldo paths (PC Gamer "faultless precision... forever"), Opus Magnum GIF export (Barth designed game to be GIF-able after player's Infinifactory GIF), Factorio "captivating" animations (RPI Polytechnic), Into the Breach clarity-over-cool principle (Justin Ma). Key insight: Robot Uprising's machine is invisible (information flow) vs. Zachtronics' visible machines (waldos, arms, belts) — signal lines must be elevated from debug overlay to primary aesthetic layer to bridge this gap. Three player journeys: Marcus 34 SWE Mission 7 full-web emergence + overload drama + flanking convergence, Priya 16 first-timer Mission 2 seeing first signal line ("Oh, it told the other one where to go"), DeepAgent_TTV 28 streamer Mission 9 eight-channel circuit-board light show with Command unit save and chat going wild. 5 new aspects discovered (1.08c-i through 1.08c-v: signal-as-GIF, audio chord system, aesthetic density ramp, overload beat drop vs crash stop, emergence gap pride/confusion). |
| 239 | 2026-03-16 | 5.14a — The fidelity threshold as onboarding gate | 1 run | Onboarding (Wave 5). Breadth-first: onboarding at 21 files (least-explored category). Designed "The Fog Mission" — the teaching moment where enemy signal flooding (fidelity 0.15-0.35) drowns player context windows, creating visceral information poisoning that manual curation can't solve. Three threshold UI variations: A "The Bouncer" (binary reject/accept, simple but no nuance), B "The Triage Nurse" (three-tier green/amber/red sorting, spectrum-aware but complex), C "The Immune System" (adaptive threshold responding to buffer pressure, most elegant but hardest to teach). Unlocks at Mission 6-7 (after factory introduction settles, requires prior understanding of confidence values, relay degradation, and rules). Key design insight: fidelity degrades ~0.12 per relay hop, so a 3-hop architecture needs threshold ≤0.5 — forcing per-blueprint threshold tuning based on position in information architecture (frontline strict, backline permissive). The threshold is the first mechanic where the player configures an *automated quality judgment* — bridge to the meta-level. Inspector's "Rejected signals" panel with gold-bordered false positives as the teaching tool for threshold calibration. Four player journeys: Sofia 34 teacher (experiences flood → learns tool → discovers false positive cost), Dev 28 backend engineer (overconfigures at 0.7 → information shadow → discovers per-blueprint tuning → maps to log levels), Marcus 14 teen (surface-level use at 0.5 → Inspector plants seed → slow-burn learning), Lena 72 retired librarian (maps to information literacy → uses FLAG mode → builds provenance mental model from Inspector). TikTok: context bar climbing red under signal flood → cut to retry with amber ember force field rejecting garbage → "teach your robots what to ignore." 5 new aspects discovered (5.14b-f: per-channel thresholds, threshold as rule condition, adaptive immune system model, enemy fidelity spoofing, overcautious diagnostic). |
| 238 | 2026-03-16 | 6.07c — Mobile-specific onboarding for touch controls | 1 run | Platform (Wave 6). Breadth-first: platform at 21 files (tied for least-explored with onboarding). Six mobile onboarding paradigms: A "Ghost Hand" (translucent animated hand overlay with gesture dictionary keyed to UI element types, language-independent, Clash Royale precedent, teaches HOW not WHY), B "Spotlight Funnel" (progressive element reveal with discovery graph DAG, prevents overwhelm, Into the Breach "highlight valid action" precedent, rails not training wheels), C "Fingerprint" (first-touch-per-element instruction with slow-mo + particles + tooltips, self-pacing, elegant degradation for desktop→mobile switchers, no sequence guarantee), D "Coach Mark Carousel" (swipeable instruction cards, beautiful format, 70-90% skip rate makes it weakest paradigm), E "The Breath" (contextual micro-pauses — 1.5s screen dim + typewriter text + diegetic boot-log aesthetic, minimal intrusion but 4-8 words can't teach complex concepts), F "The Apprentice" RECOMMENDED (adaptive four-tier hybrid: Tier 1 micro-pauses for WHAT, Tier 2 first-touch for HOW, Tier 3 ghost hand after 8s inaction, Tier 4 spotlight for mission-critical moments; per-gesture competence tracker disabling tiers at score 3/5/8; performance tier detection for budget phones). Key insight: the competence tracker makes the system self-removing — expert players never see ghost hands, casual players get full support, and the transition is invisible. Full sensory cascade description for Tier 1→2→3 on first noise drag. Comparable: Into the Breach Netflix mobile port (structural clarity over explicit teaching), Clash Royale (ghost hand gold standard — works because gesture=game concept), StS mobile (cautionary tale — no mobile-specific onboarding), Monument Valley (wordless geometry), Alto's Odyssey (continuous surface discovery). 4 player journeys (Ria 24 Manila UX designer 80s first mission on MRT, Datu 38 Cebu network engineer Factorio/Screeps vet 4-minute speedrun with system adapting out of his way, Tala 17 Batangas student Mobile Legends player needing ghost hand twice on Realme budget phone, Marcus 42 Singapore IT manager iPad Pro Into the Breach vet spending 5/7 minutes in Inspector). TikTok: "She cleaned three boxes and WON" — 12s clip of teen dragging noise slots and winning. 5 new aspects discovered (6.07c-i through 6.07c-v: ghost hand asset pipeline, competence score persistence/reset, first-touch replay, cross-device competence sync, adaptive performance tier detection). |
| 237 | 2026-03-16 | 6.01a-vii — Tile animation response to game events | 1 run | Aesthetics (Wave 6). Breadth-first: aesthetics at 21 files (tied for least-explored with onboarding, platform). Five design options: A "The Backdrop" (no event reactions, Into the Breach model — maximum readability, world as passive scenery, world's indifference as mood), B "The Footprint" (movement-only reactions — water ripples, canopy rustles, seismic tremors, neon acknowledgment; 400-800ms per biome; units have "weight" but reactions disconnected from information theme), C "The Nervous System" (signal-reactive tiles — data-lights carry channel color, bioluminescent signal wake, city fiber flash, volcanic fuse trail; EM as visible disturbance; signal overload as biome-specific crisis visual; the world IS the network; risk of "christmas tree" visual noise competing with existing signal overlays), D "The Empathy Field" (state-reactive — overload darkens tile + radiates to neighbors, tagging tints tiles cyan permanently, perception radius subtly speeds ambient animation; thematically coherent but overload competes with unit stun visual), E "The Living Stage" RECOMMENDED (curated selective reactions: Tier 1 always-active context overload tile reaction with ambient inversion + 4-neighbor radiation dimming + amber border flash, Tier 2 M5+ tag tint persistent cyan shift, Tier 3 M7+ EM emission environmental disturbance per biome; movement footprints and signal-path reactions deliberately excluded as too frequent or redundant with overlays). Selection criteria: must communicate something overlays don't, reinforce core learning, stay below gameplay events in motion hierarchy, be rare enough to feel meaningful. Per-biome EM disturbance as stealth-detection mechanic: enemy hook firings cause visible tile reactions even outside perception range. Comparable: Into the Breach (pristine tiles), Factorio (pipe flow indicators as closest analogue), ONI (overlay modes), Ori (aesthetic-only footprints). | 233 | 2026-03-16 | 1.09e — Slay the Spire 2 co-op design lessons | 1 run | Competitive analysis (Wave 1). StS2 sold 3.3M copies in first week with co-op as headline feature; 574K peak concurrent; $75M gross. Co-op architecture: parallel highway model (individual decks/gold/energy/relics, shared map path + enemies), simultaneous turn resolution, enemy HP scaling per player count, co-op exclusive cards per character class (Tag Team, Block transfer, energy donation, Legion of Bone mechanic contagion), shared debuffs benefit all players, death/revival (1 HP respawn), relic contention (RPS tiebreaker), map drawing as communication channel. Three design wins: board-game-night feeling from voting/negotiation, emergent role specialization through shared-debuff force-multiplier, difficulty scaling creating genuine interdependence. Core criticism: "parallel solitaire" — per-turn combat loop is fundamentally solo, cooperation lives between combats not during them; PC Gamer "can't see teammates' hands"; Blast.tv "three parallel solo-runs simultaneously." Community split: casual players feel it's solo-adjacent, high-Ascension players say cooperation emerges under pressure. Five cooperative models mapped: A Parallel Highway (StS2), B Shared Canvas (Robot Uprising natural fit — shared workbench/battlefield), C Divided Front (zone ownership + cross-zone channels), D Asymmetric Roles (Architect+Operator), E Blueprint Exchange (async). Five Robot Uprising translations: (1) channel wiring IS cooperative surface (every cross-player hook is collaboration), (2) Inspector as shared debugging session (pair-programming emotional register), (3) Plan screen negotiation as core loop (production queue argument = board game night), (4) sealed watch eliminates parallel solitaire structurally (no individual agency during execution), (5) difficulty scaling through signal noise volume. Four player journeys (Mei 26 + Jun roommate first co-op discovering channel naming negotiation + production queue argument, Datu 38 network engineer + Reyna building hub-and-spoke Command agent message broker on Discord, Aira 15 + kuya Marco 22 tutorial asymmetry teaching distributed perception, Prof. Santos 55 using co-op as distributed systems university lab — students independently derive Byzantine fault tolerance + quorum consensus). Six comparable cooperative games (Spirit Island asymmetric powers, Hanabi communication constraints, Keep Talking radical information asymmetry, Overcooked shared-workspace comedy, Factorio MP shared factory visibility, DOS2 disagree-and-resolve). 5 new aspects discovered (1.09e-i through 1.09e-v). |

| 227 | 2026-03-16 | 3.05a-iii — Accumulator prefix as consensus mechanic | 1 run | Building-blocks exploration (Wave 3). Breadth-first: building-blocks at 20 files (tied lowest). TALLY/TEST/QUORUM as majority voting primitive for agent decisions — "at least K of N conditions must pass." Five design variations: A "Simple Threshold" (QUORUM N, fixed integer, visible adjustable, 6 lines vs. 9 for boolean equivalent), B "Majority Keyword" (auto-calculates ⌊N/2⌋+1, self-adjusting on test count changes), C "Named Thresholds" (ANY/MAJORITY/UNANIMOUS/QUORUM N dropdown, governance vocabulary), D "Weighted Votes" (TEST(2) weighted voting, too complex for first playable, Gauntlet-era unlock), E "Rolling Accumulator" (cross-tick persistence with decay, deferred to Specialist skill). Recommended: Variation A in M6, MAJORITY alias in M7. Formal distributed systems parallel: each TEST = one vote, QUORUM = majority requirement, action = leader election outcome; Raft leader election is 1:1 mechanical parallel; Byzantine fault tolerance mapped to stale/corrupted/adversarial data tolerance (N ≥ 3F+1 generals). Visual design: silver TALLY separator with counter icon, hollow/filled/crossed vote pips (○/●/⊘) on each TEST, full-width QUORUM threshold bar with bold number above (green pulse when met, amber when not). Audio: ascending chimes per passing TEST (C→D→E), decisive fifth-chord (C-G) on quorum met, deflating minor second (E♭-D) on quorum missed, spatialized to unit grid position. | 215 | 2026-03-16 | 3.10b — Signal latency legibility | 1 run | Building-blocks exploration (Wave 3). Breadth-first: building-blocks at 18 files (tied lowest with onboarding, platform). Recommended progressive unlock: Stage 1 Feel It (M1-3, dots only), Stage 2 Count It (M4-5, pips), Stage 3 Measure It (M5-7, ruler + wavefront), Stage 4 Predict It (M6-8, ETA overlay), Stage 5 Budget It (M8-10, threat closure), Stage 6 Diagnose It (always available, deepening). Key insight from research: Factorio community built external tool (Cnide) specifically for tick-level delay visualization — when community builds external tools, the information should be in-game. TIS-100 players recommend "think like a circuit" but game doesn't help build that frame. Into the Breach proves players WANT and USE perfect consequence preview. Interaction effects: × hook chaining (hot = 0 tick cascade, cold = 1 tick per hop, ETA needs best/worst case), × EM emissions (flat fast networks = fewer hops but more direct hooks = speed-vs-stealth tradeoff), × context overload (late signals fill already-full buffers, causing eviction or stun), × vocabulary density (latency is Category B term, introduce M4-5 after player has felt it), × one-shot-one-kill (zero margin amplifies latency importance — 1 tick = life or death), × audio identity (channel motifs spaced by hop count create audible latency rhythm — "hear the network speed"), × mobile (long-press for wavefront, larger pips, horizontal scroll for long chains). Comparable: Into the Breach (consequence preview gold standard), TIS-100 (cautionary tale of implicit latency), Factorio circuits (external tool demand signal), SpaceChem (pipeline buffer as accidental latency). | 213 | 2026-03-16 | 7.03d — Workshop search and discovery UX | 1 run | Multiplayer/community exploration (Wave 7). Breadth-first: multiplayer at 18 files (tied lowest). Full design of Workshop as in-game config library — the "vinyl record shop" for attention architectures. Core problem: configs are opaque behavioral graphs, unlike Factorio blueprints (spatial/visual) or card game decks (compositional). Solution: topology diagram as visual fingerprint + X-Ray toggle as architecture schematic. Workshop Home Screen: filing-cabinet-drawer transition, 3-column card grid, 7 category tabs (Trending/New/Top Rated/Most Imported/Staff Picks/Following/For You). Config Card anatomy: hero image (replay screenshot or procedural topology diagram), signal strip (unit icons + channel count + hook density + EDT badge), tags, import/like/comment counts. Three-axis tag taxonomy: Archetype (8 tags: scout-rush through hybrid), Specialty (8 tags: anti-rush through educational), Complexity (4 tags: beginner-friendly through expert). Auto-tagging from Config Code structure + community-emergent tags via 50-use promotion threshold. Search system: full-text across decoded Config Code fields (search inside blueprints — Factorio community's biggest ask, built-in from day one) + structured query language for power users (unit:relay count:>3 skill:compress, GitHub-style qualifiers). Five-factor ranking: text relevance (0.4), popularity (0.2), recency (0.15), author reputation (0.1), match-rate (0.15). Recommendation engine: config similarity graph (4-dimensional structural fingerprint), collaborative filtering (import-to-import), skill gap detection ("New to you: uses hack skill"). Five trending models: Velocity (import acceleration), Signal-to-Noise (like:import ratio), Conversation (comment velocity), Gauntlet Disruption (meta-shift detection), Hybrid Weighted (production blend). Config similarity fingerprinting: unit composition vector + channel topology hash (isomorphic graph matching) + rule vocabulary Jaccard + skill loadout bitmask + context config signature, weighted composite score with thresholds (≥0.95 duplicate, 0.85-0.94 derivative, 0.70-0.84 related). Fork vs. plagiarism: explicit fork attribution, independent convergence transparency, no accusation — community decides. Detail page: hero replay + interactive topology diagram + X-Ray toggle (ka-chunk activation, nodes bloom open, channel pulses animate, settles to static schematic). Campaign progress gates visibility (no Command configs before M6). Import triggers Codex popups for unknown vocabulary. Comparable: Steam Workshop (card grid + sorting tabs, but mods are opaque), Factorio Prints (tag filtering but no in-game integration or blueprint search), Mario Maker Course World (Hot/Popular/New + difficulty from clear rates), hsreplay.net (archetype categories + win rates per rank, but flat card lists not topological), GitHub (stars + fork graph + README, closest to X-Ray toggle concept). 3 player journeys: Mara 14 first-timer browsing beginner-friendly tag filter → importing "Baby's First Relay Chain" → learning by tinkering with working example → following @teacherTina; Kai 28 Diamond Gauntlet vet using structured search (tag:stealth-net edt:<0.30) → X-Ray analyzing PHANTOM LATTICE → Counter Config sidebar → forking with Command agent graft → uploading hybrid with auto-attribution; Sofia 42 educator creating curated Collections for university course → structured search for educational configs → sharing Collection URL to LMS → monitoring student import/fork analytics. 5 new aspects discovered (7.03d-i through 7.03d-v: hero image generation algorithm, card-level moderation, offline mode, collection curation as content type, creator analytics dashboard). |
| 212 | 2026-03-16 | 2.00f-iii — Emergent flocking from local rules | 1 run | Core-mechanic exploration (Wave 2). Breadth-first: core-mechanic at 18 files (tied lowest). Grid quantization creates blocky movement, collision tiebreaking needs, and limited flocking range on 64-tile board. Five configurations: (1) Perception Flock "See and Follow" (zero-communication, perception-only cohesion, silent but crude on small board), (2) Signal Flock "Shout and Swarm" (per-tick heartbeat broadcasts, tightest flocking, produces flock splitting/reforming, but EM nightmare — "echo chamber" failure mode where buffer fills with heartbeats leaving no room for actual scouting data), (3) Relay-Mediated Flock "The Shepherd" (relay compresses position reports into flock-vector, most disciplined formation, 2-tick delay, relay-as-single-point-of-failure), (4) Stigmergic Flock "Leave Breadcrumbs" (tagging-based, near-silent, ant-colony pattern, dual-purpose tag/resource tension, tag decay timing critical), (5) Hybrid Flock "Whisper Network" RECOMMENDED (perception-based instant separation + 3-tick heartbeat cohesion + relay-amplified threat, three coordination layers at three speeds/costs, most naturalistic movement, best noise-to-coordination ratio). Key insight: flocking should be emergent discovery not taught mechanic — design missions rewarding distributed sensing, let players stumble onto it. Blueprint Codex flavor text plants seed without spoiling. Interaction effects: × EM emissions (tighter flock = louder signal = designed tradeoff), × one-shot-one-kill (tight flock = target-rich = separation rules are survival), × buffer model (flocking competes with mission data for 6-slot buffer — echo chamber problem), × tagging (stigmergic flock creates dual-use scout/resource tension), × campaign progression (flocking only possible M5+ when factory enables multiple scouts). Comparable: Boids (Reynolds 1986, foundational), AI War (Arcen, three-layer emergent sub-commander), Gladiabots (player surprise at own creation), Pikmin (swarm follow as emotional payload), Half-Life (literal boid code for Xen creatures). | 211 | 2026-03-16 | 5.04b — The vocabulary density curve | 1 run | Onboarding exploration (Wave 5). Cognitive load theory applied to Robot Uprising's term introduction rate. Three-tier term taxonomy: Category A "label for something you already felt" (0.25 chunks, 30-60s each — observation, noise, slot), Category B "new behavior of familiar system" (0.5-0.75 chunks, 60-120s — eviction, perception radius, amplify), Category C "fundamentally new mental model" (1.0 chunk, 2-4min — hook, channel, rule, blueprint, command agent). Five density curve models: (1) Flat Line (constant 3/mission, doesn't match locked curriculum), (2) Front Load (current design, M4 crisis at 6 terms needing 15min in 12min mission), (3) Sawtooth (alternating vocab/practice missions, breathing room but practice missions bore veterans), (4) Nintendo Curve (Kishōtenketsu 1 concept per mission, proven but requires 11 missions), (5) Adaptive (measure comprehension, adjust density per player, best outcome but highest implementation cost). Recommended: density envelope (min/max per mission) with Redistribution C — treat rule+condition+action as one compound Category C concept, pre-seed perception/skill in earlier missions as Cat A/B, reducing M4's effective load from 4C to 2C. Language acquisition research: 3-5 genuinely new concepts per 15-min learning block, 8-12 meaningful encounters for stable representation. Nintendo Kishōtenketsu: 1 mechanic per 5-min level with 4 escalating encounters. | 208 | 2026-03-16 | 6.02d — Audio identity per named channel: the Sonic Identicon | 1 run | Breadth-first: aesthetics at 17 files (tied lowest with building-blocks, onboarding, platform). Hash-to-sound pipeline: channel name → MD5/SHA-256 → 11 bytes parsed into musical parameters (root pitch, interval, waveform, attack, decay, filter cutoff, resonance, detune, reverb send, rhythmic pattern) → ~39M perceptually distinct combinations → synthesized at runtime via OscillatorNode + BiquadFilterNode + GainNode (zero audio files, zero memory footprint). Pentatonic constraint guarantees any simultaneous channel combination is harmonically safe (kulintang-compatible). Six variations: A "The Ping" (single-note identity, simplest, collision risk at scale), B "The Motif" (2-4 note melodic fragment, richer identity, temporal storytelling — signal chains become audible narratives, "ting-TING! ... nee-nah-nuh ... BWOMM" = scout→relay→striker heard as three-note call-and-response), C "The Instrument" (timbre-only variation on standard ping, easiest to parse via auditory scene analysis but lowest distinctiveness ceiling), D "The Tuning Fork" (hash default + player-configurable override via inline sound editor with pitch/texture/length/pattern controls), E "The Chorus" (traffic-adaptive mixing — busy channels louder, quiet channels fade, relay death = silence as loudest event), F "The Babel Problem" (failure mode analysis: 12+ channel saturation, name changes, co-op audio divergence, accessibility, enemy channel distortion). Recommended: B+E+D accessibility (multi-note motifs + traffic-adaptive mixing + configurable override for hash collisions + frequency floor slider for hearing loss). | 206 | 2026-03-16 | 1.08 — SpaceChem: visual programming as chemistry metaphor, production pipeline design | 1 run | Breadth-first: competitive-analysis at 21 files, all non-ui-ux categories at 17-18. Deep competitive analysis of SpaceChem (Zachtronics, 2011, $9.99, 95% Steam positive, ~5,050 reviews, 1-2M owners, Metacritic 84, <2% completion rate). Core loop: place instructions on 10×8 grid → program two waldos (red/blue) → watch simulation → crash → debug → iterate. KEY TRANSLATIONS: dual-waldo system = multi-agent coordination (two agents, shared space, one Sync primitive creates emergent behavior), production pipeline = factory + channel architecture (reactor=blueprint, pipe=channel, throughput balancing=signal latency management), pipeline buffer length = relay context window sizing (emergent vs. designed buffering), histogram = locked Robot Uprising feature (antagonistic axes: cycles×symbols×reactors). Instruction set: ~15 commands (Grab, Drop, Rotate, Bond+/-, Sense, Fuse, Fission, Sync, Flip-Flop, Input/Output) — matches Zachtronics 12-15 primitive sweet spot, same as Robot Uprising's 4 primitive types × skills. Five things SpaceChem gets CATASTROPHICALLY wrong: (1) all instructions unlocked from start (vs. Robot Uprising's progressive unlock), (2) binary success/failure with no degree-of-failure feedback (vs. Inspector + near-miss rendering), (3) no hint system (vs. boot log + animated tooltips), (4) story gated behind difficulty (vs. diegetic boot log), (5) single save slot per puzzle (vs. config versioning). The dual-waldo system is the closest existing model for Robot Uprising's multi-agent hook coordination — Sync as barrier primitive maps directly to blocking hook semantics; implicit timing-based coordination (no Sync needed) maps to well-designed asynchronous channel architectures. Production levels as cognitive upgrade: single-reactor→pipeline transition creates permanent shift from component-thinking to system-thinking, directly parallels M1-4→M5 factory transition. The "running machine" moment — watching synchronized waldos flow through a working pipeline — is the aesthetic target for sealed watch. Pipeline buffering as accidental context window: players discovered longer pipes absorb throughput mismatches, analogous to relay units buffering signal flow. Community: optimization archetypes (cycle/symbol/reactor optimizers), ResearchNet custom puzzles as proto-Workshop, Community Edition open-source mods, PAX tournaments. | 168 | 2026-03-16 | 3.05a — Conditional prefix as expressive primitive | 1 run | Deep dive into the +/− prefix as the minimal vocabulary for agent behavioral logic. The primitive itself (TEST sets flag, + fires on true, − fires on false) yields boolean composition without explicit AND/OR/NOT operators: AND from chaining +TEST lines, OR from repeated action blocks, NOT from the − prefix. Six variations explored: (A) Binary Toggle (Shenzhen I/O baseline, three symbols), (B) Tri-State (+ − ? with ? for missing data), (C) Accumulator (counter-based majority voting, maps to Raft/Paxos consensus), (D) Named Flag (multi-flag variables, minimal register machine), (E) Decay Flag (temporal confidence degradation, maps to TTL), (F) Prefix Palette RECOMMENDED (progressive unlock: · only M1-2 → +/− M3-4 → ? M5 → chained composition M6-7 → JUMP M8 → full expression M9-10). Four-stage teaching sequence maps exactly to 10-mission campaign. ARM conditional predication parallel: Robot Uprising's in-order tick evaluation makes predication permanently optimal (ARM dropped it in AArch64 only because OoO made branch prediction cheap). Detailed UI spec: 12×12 prefix toggle with click-to-cycle micro-animations (tik/tok/hmm/pff sounds), yellow-gold TEST line highlights at 8% opacity, evaluation waterfall animation (200-400ms top-to-bottom during sealed watch). Interaction effects: buffer-native (every TEST reads context window), hooks (signals become decision inputs), EM emissions (skipped instructions emit zero noise = stealth mechanic within rules), Inspector (linear trace is trivially debuggable), Command agents (14-slot buffers need deepest prefix logic). Comparable: Shenzhen I/O +/− prefix (direct ancestor), ARM predication (1985-present), Gladiabots behavior trees (visual hierarchy vs. linear compactness tradeoff), production rule systems (OPS5/CLIPS/Drools), assembly language conditional execution. 3 player journeys: Lena 13 first-timer discovering +/− through traffic-light tooltip animation and guided fill-in-the-blank, Raj 35 Go engineer implementing backpressure via prefix rate-limiting on relay (goroutine scheduling parallel), Abuela Carmen 67 retired teacher understanding prefix as traffic light pattern ("yellow asks, green goes, orange waits"). | 156 | 2026-03-15 | 6.01a-i — Tile animation budget | 1 run | Per-biome animation specs for all 5 locked biomes (Ifugao rice terraces, Siquijor mystic island, Palawan jungle, Cebu/Manila cyberpunk city, Taal volcano). "Breathe, Don't Dance" philosophy: no tile animation cycle under 2s, establishing 6-level motion hierarchy (combat flash fastest → tile animation slowest). Per-biome specs: terraces (water shimmer 0.25Hz + data-light pulse 0.125Hz + mist drift 0.0625Hz), Siquijor (bioluminescent pulse 0.25Hz ± jitter + coral shimmer 0.125Hz + root shadow sway 0.0625Hz), jungle (canopy shadow drift 0.0625Hz + bamboo sway 0.125Hz + flower color cycle 0.0417Hz), city (neon flicker 0.5Hz wall-face-only + fiber optic glow 0.25Hz + laundry 0.0625Hz + street traffic 0.125Hz), Taal (lava glow 0.25Hz + steam vent bursts 6-10s variable + heat distortion full-tile + ember drift 0.0833Hz). All biomes target 1.0-2.4% of visible pixels animated per frame. Pixi.js rendering budget: hybrid approach (static base tilemap + animated overlay tilemap = 2 draw calls total); 0.3-0.8ms additional over static tiles within 16.67ms frame budget; ~3.75MB total VRAM trivial for any hardware. Overlay suppression system: when gameplay events activate on a tile, top-surface animations dim to 20% intensity over 200ms, restore over 500ms; wall-face animations never suppressed; Taal exception (never suppresses — final mission earns the right to overwhelm). Three performance tiers: Tier 1 full atmosphere, Tier 2 reduced motion (doubled cycles, disabled mist/steam/heat distortion, 32 animated tiles), Tier 3 static terrain (maps to accessibility reduce-motion). Playwright determinism via injectable animation clock override. "The Comprehension Cliff" named pattern: exponential cognitive load from interacting rules (3 rules = trivial, 8+ = overwhelming), Baba's solution = branching paths to skip, Robot Uprising's solution = Inspector for navigating combinatorial space. | 145 | 2026-03-15 | 5.11b — The corrupted diff as endgame adversarial escalation | 1 run | Five approaches to diff compromise: (A) "Baseline Poisoning" (enemy overwrites clean reference to match corrupted doc, diff computes zero differences, silent alarm problem when audio piggybacks on diff output), (B) "Selective Blind Spot" (diff catches 2/3 corruptions, false-clean signal more dangerous than total failure — player's successful use of tool becomes the trap), (C) "Inverted Diff" (baseline contains enemy values, diff marks correct text as corrupted, RESTORE makes document worse — gaslighting problem requiring secondary verification), (D) "Time Bomb" (diff correct during Plan, corrupted retroactively during transition to Inspector — temporal confusion compounding with Living Document Model 6), (E) "Confidence Attack" (probabilistic confidence percentage replacing binary result, 85%→40% degradation across missions, time-pressure verification tradeoff). "VERIFY AGAINST MISSION HISTORY" tool introduced M9 as secondary verification bypassing corruptible baseline — compares document against actual deployed configs from past victories; doubles as accessibility feature for players with memory difficulties. "No tool is infallible" as deepest pedagogical claim: maps to supply chain attacks (A), incomplete test coverage (B), compromised rollbacks (C), log tampering (D), model confidence calibration (E). Specialist baseline recovery as potential mid-mission tactical objective. | 142 | 2026-03-15 | 3.09 — Hook chaining: cascade effects, infinite loops, back pressure | 1 run | Six approaches explored: (A) "The Filing Cabinet" — no chaining, hooks fire from world events only, ON_RECEIVE doesn't exist, zero cascade risk but latency kills responsiveness (Scout→Relay→Striker = 3+ ticks), Relays become passive mail sorters; (B) "The Circuit Board" — same-tick chaining with configurable depth limit (default 8), produces electric "lightning through the network" sealed watch visuals but creates infinite loop risk, cascade ordering ambiguity, and EM emission bursts; (C) "The Relay Race" — delayed chaining (1 tick per hop, matches locked spec), cascades visible as slow domino chains, self-limiting loops (ping-pong costs 2 ticks/oscillation), but incentivizes flat architectures to minimize latency; (D) "The Fuse" — hybrid depth-limited with per-unit chain budget (Scout:1, Relay:3, Command:4), graceful degradation (queued not dropped), but adds another number to track per unit; (E) "The Spark Gap" — conditional chaining via hot/cold toggle per hook (🔥 instant cascade + 2× EM vs ❄️ buffer-only + silent), player explicitly designs speed-vs-stealth trade-off per signal path; (F) "The Awakening" RECOMMENDED — progressive chaining across campaign (M1-3 no chaining → M4 ON_RECEIVE cold only → M5-6 delayed chaining → M7 hot mode unlocked → M8-10 full chaining with chain budgets). Audio vocabulary: cold=soft blip, hot=metallic tick, deep cascade=rapid tick→electronic sizzle, budget exhaustion=descending bwwwp, loop detection=harsh bzzt. | 135 | 2026-03-15 | 1.17 — Into the Breach: perfect information tactics, consequence preview, small-scale precision | 1 run | The most-referenced game in Robot Uprising's locked design decisions. Metacritic 90, ~533K Steam sales, $14.99 no-MTX. Core mechanic: perfect information on 8×8 isometric grid — every enemy telegraphs attacks, every player action previews consequences. "Sacrifice cool ideas for clarity every time" (Justin Ma). Animated tooltip breakthrough: showing weapon effects via micro-simulation eliminated text confusion. Positional play over damage — push/pull repositioning maps to Robot Uprising's information routing. Power Grid (7 shared HP = buildings) creates cascading erosion stakes. Squad system (14 squads, each dramatically different playstyle) drives replayability via achievement-gated unlocks. Steel Judoka (zero-damage squad, pure repositioning) is closest existing gameplay to Robot Uprising's attention-architecture philosophy. Advanced Edition (2022, free) added 5 squads, 40 weapons, Unfair mode. Key criticisms: "puzzle not strategy" (determinism), repetitiveness post-mastery, zero modding support (cautionary tale). Robot Uprising inverts the information model (perfect player information → imperfect agent information) while inheriting grid size, visual clarity, snap movement, one-shot lethality. 3 player journeys: Marcus 35 SWE chain-reaction repositioning (push Vek into another's attack), Aisha 22 first-timer learning through consequence preview (UI teaches triage without text), Dr. Tanaka 58 chess player Steel Judoka Unfair sacrifice calculation. 5 new aspects discovered (1.17a–e: animated tooltip pattern, consequence preview gap, positional-to-informational play mapping, Tactical Breach Wizards lineage, Steel Judoka as prototype). |

State variant derivation: destroyed=40% vertical collapse + desaturate + spark + vine reclamation (Philippine jungle reclaiming metal), ghost=40% opacity + cyan tint + scan line. Enemy faction uses gunmetal body + uniform red accents + faster animations creating subliminal aggression. Portraits (128×128) carry SE Asian cultural details: Baybayin etchings on Scout dome, bolo-curved Striker blades, T'nalak weaving patterns on Relay dish, fisherman multi-tool Specialist implements. Buffer bar width as secondary identification cue (6<8<10<12<14 pips). | 130 | 2026-03-15 | 2.01 — Fixed-slot buffer: N discrete slots, oldest evicted first | 1 run | The baseline buffer model: N-slot FIFO queue per unit (Scout 6, Striker 8, Relay 12, Specialist 10, Command 14). Each slot holds one datum {source, channel, signal_type, payload, tick_created, tick_received}. Simultaneous arrivals ordered deterministically (clockwise observations first, alphabetical channels second). Buffer fills from perception + hook messages; oldest evicted when full. The "eviction flash" (red pip at left edge of buffer bar) as the game's most important micro-animation — visible during Sealed Watch, diagnostic in Inspector. Creates central strategic tension: managing information flow rate upstream (channel subscriptions, Relay intermediaries, unit selection) rather than eviction policy downstream. Key patterns discovered: context window catastrophe (buffer completely cycles in one tick), producer-consumer bottleneck (Relay drowning in Scout input faster than compress can process), stale cache problem (Command acting on old data, solved by engineering TTL via heartbeat polling — which itself creates buffer pressure, the self-defeating fix), horizontal scaling (splitting one Relay into Relay-West + Relay-East = load balancing). Comparable: TIS-100 2-register nodes (blocks on full vs. Robot Uprising drops on full), Screeps 2MB global memory (global pool vs. per-unit buffer), Gladiabots tag system (1-bit memory vs. structured data packets), real LLM context windows (128K-200K tokens as scaled-up version of same mechanic), Into the Breach (shared constraint-driven decision pattern). 4 player journeys: Mei 14 first-timer discovers hook messages don't trigger observation-based rules + first eviction flash encounter, Diego 31 backend engineer discovers dropped-packet/horizontal-scaling analogy through Relay bottleneck, Priya 47 CS teacher discovers stale-cache/TTL problem and maps to OS page replacement curriculum, Kwame 27 streamer's "Hydra" architecture collapses under enemy factory scaling + "brainfried" community vocabulary emerges. 5 new aspects discovered (2.01a–e: insertion order opacity, transit eviction, empty slots as strategy, buffer bars as spectator tool, polling vs event-driven). |

Major weaknesses: latency (~100ms per agent needed at 1s ticks), cost (~$2.9M/yr at 100K players on cloud API), non-determinism breaks Gauntlet competitive play, model updates break save files, "just write better prompts" frustration spiral, debugging ambiguity (config fault vs. hallucination). Prompt engineering IS the skill ceiling — expert players discover that terse prompts are both cheaper and more reliable. 3 player journeys: Maya 16yo hitting token cliff (agents literally run out of thought mid-sentence), Raj 34 ML engineer optimizing relay compression ratios (relay costs more tokens than it saves in simple cases), Tomoko 72 retiree writing "walk around and tell me if you see enemies" (never told agent to survive → agent dies). 5 new aspects discovered (2.00d-i local vs cloud inference, 2.00d-ii temperature as difficulty, 2.00d-iii prompt injection as enemy mechanic, 2.00d-iv model selection as progression, 2.00d-v hallucination trust problem). |

| 112 | 2026-03-15 | 6.08 — Accessibility: colorblind modes, screen reader support, difficulty options, one-handed play | 1 run | Comprehensive six-axis accessibility exploration: Visual axis (3 colorblind approaches — A "Palette Swap" simple remapping, B "Full Customization" per-element picker, C "Shape-First Design" RECOMMENDED where every color-coded element has non-color differentiator by default; low-vision magnification 0.5x-4x board zoom + 80-200% text scaling + high-contrast mode true black/white/yellow + focus-follows-selection auto-scroll; screen reader parallel DOM using PixiJS 8.x accessibility overlay system, board cells as role="gridcell" with unit/buffer/hook/terrain state, workbench natively React DOM, production queue keyboard-reorderable; Sealed Watch narration 3 options — A tick-by-tick verbose, B event-only concise, C hybrid RECOMMENDED with event-only default + Space dashboard + detail toggle; Inspector fully DOM-navigable with trend descriptions for charts). Auditory axis (10-element visual replacement table for every audio cue — tick flash, signal chevron, combat X-mark, buffer shake, EM rings, etc.; auditory processing disorder mode with 5-category simplification + sound prioritization slider). Motor axis (A mouse-only mode via right-click context menus + on-screen arrow buttons for reordering — nearly free to implement; B one-handed keyboard presets clustered for left-hand WASD or right-hand arrows; C adaptive input with switch scanning, eye tracker dwell-to-click, voice control via VoiceAttack/Windows Voice Access — aspirational). Cognitive axis (A "Complexity Layers" Simple/Standard/Full toggle — Simple hides channel map, EM overlay, fidelity numbers, payload config, showing ~40% of full information; B "Adaptive Complexity" auto-disclose based on interaction tracking; RECOMMENDED: A as explicit setting + B as soft guidance within chosen ceiling; dyslexia: OpenDyslexic font option, adjustable line/letter spacing, icon-first design pairing every condition/action text with icon, lightweight TTS via browser SpeechSynthesis; ADHD: quick-setup templates, session timer, auto-save every interaction, 1-second tick pace as micro-event rhythm matching attention refresh, Inspector bookmark/pin system; autism: 4-level animation/sound/shake intensity controls Off/Reduced/Standard/Enhanced, flash reduction, predictability via preview mode and consistent layout). Difficulty (Celeste-model Assist Mode: tick speed 0.25-2x, buffer/perception bonus, enemy speed/perception reduction, hint frequency slider — zero judgment, no achievements disabled, no "Easy Mode" label; Practice Sandbox unlocked Mission 2). Intersectional testing requirements (blind+motor switch scanning + auto-narration; deaf+colorblind shapes + visual replacements; cognitive+low-vision reduced info + magnification synergy). Key insight: Robot Uprising is UNIQUELY accessible among strategy games because its core decisions (buffer config, rule ordering, hook wiring) are abstract/structural not spatial/twitch — a blind player's channel topology reasoning is mechanically identical to a sighted player's. The Sealed Watch's "no tools" rule is an accessibility asset (zero interactions to make accessible during passive phase). PixiJS accessibility system creates invisible DOM overlays aligned to canvas objects, supporting Tab navigation and ARIA labels — the engineering foundation exists in the framework. Screen reader experience description: "NVDA's flat synthetic voice IS accidentally perfect casting for an AI protagonist reading its own boot log." Comparable: Celeste (Assist Mode gold standard, copy tone and granularity), Slay the Spire (community mods Say the Spire/TextTheSpire/InSpire proved blind strategy game demand exists — ship built-in not mod-dependent), TLoU2 (60+ options set industry ceiling, proved commercial viability), Into the Breach (structural accessibility from grid+discrete ticks inherited by Robot Uprising), Hades (narratively integrated God Mode). Interaction effects: priority-queue rules most accessible rules language (linear list for screen readers, arrow-button reorder for motor); node-graph building blocks LEAST accessible (spatial reasoning + precise mouse mandatory — needs parallel list alternative); Simple mode should default for M1-2 matching game's teaching progression; Sealed Watch 1-second ticks ADHD-friendly; Assist Mode in ranked: buffer bonus doesn't improve DESIGN quality, game tests information architecture not reflexes, ESA AGI standard pushes toward universal competitive access. TikTok clip: blind player screen reader playthrough — "designed for screen readers from day one." | 111 | 2026-03-14 | 5.02 — Tutorial as narrative: story-driven introduction, AI waking up | 1 run | Six executions of the locked boot-log tutorial mapped: A "The Cold Start" (pure text boot sequence — 45s of teal monospace, subsystem-by-subsystem ONLINE, board resolves from static, maximum diegetic purity but emotionally flat), B "The First Thought" (AI self-discovery internal monologue — "I can see. There is a grid." — contemplative, 90s to first interaction, emotional power but writing-quality-dependent and slow), C "The Subsystem Chorus" (four voices — CORE/PERCEPTION/CONTEXT/HOOK BUS — each teaching its own primitive, HOOK BUS's lonely→ecstatic arc as micro-narrative, rich audio identity per voice but four simultaneous text streams can overwhelm), D "The Terminal Session" (interactive CLI — player types `activate perception`, terminal-to-GUI metamorphosis as the tutorial itself, active rather than passive but typing hostile to console/mobile and contradicts locked "NOT freeform text" constraint), E "The Diagnostic Replay" (opens with a FAILED battle replay — frozen striker, clogged buffer — player diagnoses before building, "show don't tell" extreme, teaches Inspector first but confusing without context), F "The Hybrid Awakening" RECOMMENDED (Cold Start 15s → First Thought 30s → Subsystem Chorus M2-3 → Diagnostic Replay M2 debrief → Fade M4+, using each execution's strength at the moment it matters). Key insight: the AI's fictional awakening and the player's learning are THE SAME EVENT — not parallel, identical. Portal's GLaDOS solved "when does the game start?" by making the tutorial feel like the game; Robot Uprising solves it by making the tutorial BE the AI's first moments of consciousness. The Hybrid's emotional arc maps to learning stages: awe (I exist) → curiosity (what can I do?) → failure (that didn't work) → mastery (I understand now). Narrative voices must fade — tutorial success measured by its own disappearance. 4 player journeys: Tomás 16 first-strategy-game (AI uncertainty mirrors his own, learns through aligned confusion), Dr. Priya 38 ML lead (maps vocabulary to professional context in 25 seconds, verifies game's technical credibility), Marcus 52 history teacher (primary-source-analysis metaphor for buffer filtering, learns through domain transfer), Anika 12 Minecraft builder (skips all text, learns entirely through drag-and-watch interaction, narrative invisible but functional as safety net). | 110 | 2026-03-14 | 3.08 — Hook taxonomy: event vocabulary and action grammar of inter-agent wiring | 1 run | Two independent design axes mapped: trigger event vocabularies (5 approaches) and payload models (5 approaches), creating a 5×5 design matrix. Trigger approaches: A "Minimal" (5 events — observe/threat/receive/eliminate/skill — tutorial-only, ceiling at Mission 3), B "Typed" (12 events — spot_enemy/spot_ally/threat_enter/threat_exit/receive_signal/buffer_full/evade/engage/breach_start/compress/hack/idle — sweet spot for 10-mission campaign, distinguishes events that matter for strategy), C "Parameterized" (8 base triggers × N params — ON_PERCEIVE(enemy_striker, north, close), ON_SILENCE("feed", 3), ON_TICK(5) — kitchen-faucet model, absence detection revolutionary, periodic behaviors, but 16-24 decisions per agent), D "Compound" (boolean AND/OR/NOT composition of triggers — maximum expressiveness, NOT enables kill-switch pattern, but De Morgan's law and debugging nightmare), E "Hybrid Progressive" RECOMMENDED (5 layers unlocked across campaign: minimal M1-2 → typed M3-4 → parameterized M5-6 → AND-only M7-8 → full compound M9-10). Payload models: M1 "Implicit" (trigger event forwarded verbatim — zero config, natural learning through Inspector discovery, but no abstraction), M2 "Tagged" (6 signal types ALERT/REPORT/REQUEST/COMMAND/STATUS/INTELLIGENCE with color coding and variable buffer cost — visual language, rule integration, emergent economy, but fixed vocabulary), M3 "Structured" (player selects fields: position, target_type, tick, direction, buffer_state, signal_chain — pure data engineering lesson, "API schema design as gameplay"), M4 "Template" (string interpolation with placeholders — self-documenting human-readable signals, but text input breaks visual paradigm), M5 "Layered" RECOMMENDED (auto default → stripped fields M3+ → tagged+annotated M5+ — progressive disclosure, zero-config start, "aha when stripping fields fixes buffer overflow" as best teaching moment). Recommended combination: E×M5 (Hybrid Progressive × Layered Payloads) — both halves grow in parallel with campaign. EM emission dimension: narrow triggers + stripped payloads = stealth ("dark network" as advanced strategy); broad triggers + rich payloads = intelligence but vulnerability. 3 player journeys: Kai 25 full-stack dev first hook wiring (discovers plumbing metaphor, relay buffer overflow as first real failure, 4-minute learning arc), Amara 38 project manager (parameterized triggers = email filters aha, invents ON_SILENCE watchdog from professional metaphor), Yuto 16 competitive gamer (compound triggers + stripped payloads = dark network stealth architecture, screenshots EM overlay for Discord guide). Cross-game synthesis table (Gladiabots, Factorio, Screeps, IFTTT/Zapier, Shenzhen I/O, Into the Breach, Dwarf Fortress). | 109 | 2026-03-14 | 3.05 — Rules language: the grammar of agent behavior | 1 run | Six approaches to the rules language mapped across the structured→expressive spectrum: A "Dispatch Table" (fixed signal→action lookup, zero syntax, accessible in under 60s, ceiling at Mission 3 — no composition, no spatial reasoning, no multi-action; pairs well with rich hook system but cripples Command agents), B "Priority Queue" (ordered condition→action pairs with filter pills for age/fidelity/count/distance — literal locked spec, Gladiabots-proven, first-match-wins semantics, ordering IS the strategy, filters add depth additively, natural campaign pacing from conditions-only → filters → count≥N; weaknesses: sequential thinking required, +/− prefix cryptic until learned, JUMP creates spaghetti, alienates non-programmers), D "Sentence Builder" (Baba Is You tile-dragging onto sentence rails — grammar-enforced: snap points only accept valid tile categories, color-coded word types, auto-generated English readout below each rail; tile unlock = vocabulary expansion = tutorial; "Scrabble for robots" feel; weaknesses: finite vocabulary ceiling, screen-hungry horizontal rails, grammar design is the complexity ceiling, touch-hostile), E "Nested Conditional" (full behavior tree with Selector/Sequence/Condition/Action/Decorator nodes — maximum expressiveness, visual evaluation trace during sealed watch shows decision path, professional-grade 1:1 with game AI tools; weaknesses: Selector vs Sequence semantics non-obvious, spatial layout consumes workbench, mouse-intensive, controller-hostile), F "Pattern Matcher" (buffer-template matching where rule = "when buffer looks like THIS → do THIS" — buffer-native, visual-thinker-friendly, ratio/count first-class; weaknesses: unusual with no comparable game, near-misses invisible, pattern explosion for coverage). Two hybrid recommendations: "Growing Grammar" (dispatch table M1-2 → priority queue M3-4 → sentence builder M5-7 → full expression M8-10, parallels Python→C→Assembly teaching) or "Dual-Mode" (priority queue default + assembly prefix as unlockable Advanced Mode, both compile to same evaluation engine). Key insight from Keiko journey: Priority Queue's FLATNESS forces players to push modal logic into the relay architecture (inventing Observer pattern), making the limitation a feature that teaches distributed systems design. Key insight from Kwame journey: military veterans map priority ordering to FRAGO (fragmentary orders) — contact reports override standing orders = higher priority rules override lower. 11 player journeys across all approaches spanning ages 10-61 and experience levels from first-game-ever to Shenzhen I/O top 10%. | 107 | 2026-03-14 | 5.06 — Failure and recovery: what happens when you lose a mission | 1 run | Seven failure recovery options explored: A "Clean Slate" (zero-penalty instant retry — Opus Magnum/Baba Is You model, maximum learning velocity but zero stakes), B "Debrief Gate" (Inspector engagement required before retry unlocks — teaches debrief habit but patronizing for veterans), C "Invisible Randomization Reveal" (same config new seed reveals structural vs. scenario failure — teaches robustness but obscures controlled experimentation; hybrid RETRY + NEW RUN recommended from M5+), D "Sacrifice System" (material/intel cost per failure — real stakes but downward spiral risk; EXCLUDED from recommendation), E "Mentor System" (progressive hints: gold diamond at attempt 2 → tooltip at 3 → suggestion at 4 → Show Fix at 5 → Watch Solution at 6 — meets every player where they are, prevents quit cliff, dismissible), F "Timeline Branch" (narrative framing of retries as parallel timelines in boot log — zero-cost narrative enrichment, archive as learning tool), G "Escape Hatch" (skip mission after 5 failures, max 2 skips, skipped missions remain accessible). RECOMMENDATION: six-layer system — Layer 1 Clean Slate foundation (free retries always), Layer 2 Debrief Nudge (auto-scroll to critical tick, gold diamond after 2nd failure), Layer 3 New Seed (from M5+), Layer 4 Mentor Hints (progressive from attempt 3+), Layer 5 Timeline Narrative (cosmetic boot log enrichment), Layer 6 Escape Hatch (after 5 failures, max 2 skips). Sacrifice system explicitly excluded — penalizing failure penalizes learning. Four-tier failure hierarchy defined: Tier 0 partial success, Tier 1 mission failure, Tier 2 repeated failure (3+), Tier 3 campaign stall. 4 player journeys: Tomás 16 (4 attempts at M4, gold diamonds guide channel filtering discovery, 8 min total), Priya 34 ML engineer (2 attempts at M7, uses NEW RUN for robustness testing, discovers stale-data problem, adds signal_age rule), Kai 11 (6 attempts at M5, hint system catches him at attempt 5 with Show Fix, modifies suggestion, learns production ordering), Dr. Amara 52 low-vision (2 attempts at M3, case-sensitive channel name typo reveals accessibility surface). Comparable: Into the Breach (granular loss + timeline narrative), XCOM 2 (what NOT to do — downward spirals), Slay the Spire (knowledge-as-progression), Opus Magnum (zero-penalty baseline), Celeste (transparent assist mode), Outer Wilds (death as mechanic, knowledge persists). Interaction effects: sealed watch MUST play on every retry (emotional processing), Inspector IS the failure recovery mechanism, sandbox intermissions provide natural pressure release, filter tutorial trains Inspector habit, mentor hints create implicit dynamic difficulty. Breadth-first: campaign had 3 files tied for least coverage. 5 new aspects discovered (5.06a–e: attempt counter as social signal, failure audio agung strike, channel naming accessibility, RESUPPLY safety net, skip-mission compressed pedagogy). |

| 93 | 2026-03-14 | 4.69e-i-a-i-f-i-α-i-A-α-i-1-I-a-i-A-i-a-i-a-i — Commit-msg hook enforcement strategy | 1 run | Six design options for enforcing structured git trailers on allowlist-modifying commits. Option A Pure Warning (yellow banner, exit 0, zero friction, warning fatigue risk). Option B Warn-Then-Block with adoption timer (countdown to enforcement date, config file controls mode, cliff-edge transition). Option C Soft Block with env var override (L10N_SKIP_TRAILER_CHECK=1 disables only this check not all hooks, documented intent in shell history, override creep risk). Option D Hard Block no documented override (maximum compliance, --no-verify knowledge asymmetry, collateral damage disabling all hooks). Option E Smart Gate with JSON diff parsing (analyzes staged diff semantically — expires/renewals changes require full trailer set, reason-only changes require minimum set, whitespace-only changes pass freely; eliminates primary override case; 200+ line script complexity, JSON parsing latency concern). Option F CI-Only enforcement no local hook (zero local setup, handles squash-merge, late feedback loop, no teaching moment at commit time). Recommendation: Phase 1 Option A (pure warning weeks 1-2) → Phase 2 Option E (Smart Gate with Option C env var as emergency bypass) → Always Option F as CI backstop. Three positions on --no-verify auditing (Position 1 trust CI, Position 2 local bypass log via post-commit hook, Position 3 make bypass unnecessary via golden path — Position 3 recommended as primary strategy with Position 1 as acceptance of reality). Hook stacking problem: multiple commit-msg hooks create whack-a-mole blocking pattern driving developers to --no-verify. Solution: hook compositor pattern — single dispatcher runs all checks, reports ALL failures simultaneously, enables context sharing (secret scanner aware of trailer regions). Husky integration for React+Vite stack. Interaction effects: shared validation logic between hook and CI (must agree on valid trailer set), make renew-allowlist CLI tool as golden path making hook irrelevant for tool users, squash-merge trailer preservation (sibling aspect), defer verdict special warning, cross-tool trailer namespace, CI auto-renewal bypasses local hooks entirely. Three player journeys: Aarav (28 platform engineer — third renewal via make renew-allowlist, chooses defer verdict, CI advisory surfaces consecutive renew→renew→defer pattern, escalates INTL-847 via Slack screenshot); Priya (24 junior frontend — fixes typo in reason field, Smart Gate detects text-only change requiring minimum trailers, uses env var override, CI classifies as advisory not blocking, learns convention from Aarav's PR review comment); Dev (35 staff engineer — debugs hook conflict where secret scanner false-positives on concatenated trailers, implements compositor pattern with context sharing, adds exclusion for Allowlist-* regions). Comparable: ESLint --fix with lint-staged (hook suggests fix command pattern), commitlint (structural analog for commit format enforcement), Chromium Bug:/Change-Id trailers (auto-generate some + require human input for others), GitHub required status checks (CI-only enforcement model), Terraform plan-before-apply (analyze diff before enforcing rules). Five new sub-aspects discovered: hook compositor architecture (a-i-a), Smart Gate JSON diff parser design (a-i-b), hook installation and distribution via Husky/lefthook (a-i-c), shared validation library between hook and CI (a-i-d), Allowlist-Tool provenance trailer for tool-generated commits (a-i-e). |

Option A: Help-Text Snapshot Diffing (--help output as committed snapshot, diff against current; simple but over-sensitive to formatting changes). Option B: AST-Extracted Argument Manifest (parse argument parser source code; semantic comparison but fragile extraction, language-dependent). Option C: Argument Manifest as Source of Truth (schema-first — generate parser from JSON schema; drift-proof by construction but requires architectural inversion). Option D: Runtime Argument Enumeration (--enumerate-args flag exposing structured JSON; language-agnostic, runtime-accurate, proximity-based drift reduction). Option E: Cross-Tool Integration Test (round-trip verifier — audit generates command, repair executes in dry-run; catches ALL three drift types but requires test fixtures). Recommendation: Option D + Option E as two-tier check — Tier 1 (PR-blocking, <500ms): --enumerate-args manifest comparison with auto-classification of breaking vs. non-breaking changes; Tier 1.5 (PR-blocking): bidirectional template completeness check (repair→audit AND audit→repair after Dev's phantom flag discovery); Tier 2 (nightly, 5-10s): round-trip integration test. Three-way sync problem identified: repair tool parser, audit tool command template, and version constant must all stay synchronized — three tiers each target one sync gap. Auto-classification rules match parent's version bump protocol (new required flag = BREAKING, new optional flag = NON-BREAKING). Co-location principle: --enumerate-args output, parser, and version constant all within first 50 lines of source file — proximity reduces forgetting better than any automated check. Three player journeys: Aarav (CI engineer — Jin's PR caught by Tier 1.5 because new --strategy flag added to repair tool but not audit template; learns three-tier model), Priya (L10n maintainer — adds optional --verbose flag, Tier 1 auto-classifies as NON-BREAKING, no version bump required, zero friction), Dev (junior engineer — discovers phantom flag blind spot when nightly round-trip catches removed --validate-budget still in audit template; implements reverse direction check for Tier 1.5). | 88 | 2026-03-14 | 4.69e-x — Tag evidence export for community discussion | 1 run | Five design options for shareable analytical artifacts showing match-source breakdown and tagging rationale without revealing opponent identity. Core problem: adversarial tagging is a solitary diagnostic skill that community discussion could accelerate — but exporting match-source data creates privacy minefields (deanonymization, witch hunts, harassment). Option A: Evidence Card — static 600×800px PNG with anonymized concentration bar, before/after cluster comparison, free-text annotation (280 chars), community tag count/conviction score, shareable URL; dark-slate career-analysis aesthetic; privacy-preserving but cherry-pickable and deanonymizable in small brackets. Option C: Redacted Report — PDF-style structured document with declassified-intelligence aesthetic (cream background, monospace font, heavy black ████ redaction bars, Executive Summary→Evidence→Analysis→Recommendation structure); aesthetic filters for analytical audience; concentration trend over time as temporal evidence of escalating targeting; format friction limits adoption to engaged diagnosticians. Option E: Progressive Disclosure Hybrid (RECOMMENDED) — four layers coexisting: Snippet (auto, one tap) → Evidence Card (auto, review before share) → Redacted Report (player-written) → Diagnostic Notebook (player-curated, interactive); each layer links to next; matches communication contexts (chat/Reddit/wiki/deep-dive); privacy gradients scale with disclosure level. Comparable systems: Opus Magnum GIF export (self-contained visual artifact, single-player so no privacy concern — lesson: artifacts must be legible without game context), Lichess shareable URLs (frictionless generation, both players public — lesson: friction kills adoption), VALORANT SNAP//SHOT (self-expression not evidence, only sharer's data — lesson: format for social media matters), Spotify Wrapped (surveillance-as-entertainment, privacy paradox — lesson: frame export around sharer's experience not subject's behavior), Chess.com PGN export (structured data for analytical communities — lesson: analytical communities prefer structured formats over visual cards). Unique constraint: Robot Uprising evidence exports necessarily involve ANOTHER player's behavioral data, anonymized — no comparable system shares relational analytical data this way. | 87 | 2026-03-14 | 4.69e-i-a-i-f-i-α-i-A-α-i-1-I-a-i-A — `repair_command_version` field for schema compatibility | 1 run | Five design options for versioning repair command format in audit JSON output (A integer counter — RECOMMENDED, B semver, C capability set, D content-addressed hash, E timestamp-only). Core problem: cached audit JSON contains `repair_command` strings that embed the repair tool's argument schema; if the schema changes (argument renamed, mandatory flag added), cached commands silently fail at `eval` time. Integer counter chosen for simplicity (one constant, `!=` comparison, trivially debuggable). Version bump protocol: mandatory arg changes = bump, optional flag additions = no bump (old commands remain valid). Dual placement: `audit_metadata.repair_command_schema_version` for whole-file consumers + per-entry `repair_command_version` for `jq`-extracted entries. Stale-command error path designed with actionable message (expected vs actual version, re-run command, "what changed" changelog excerpt). Cache invalidation strategy for three cache surfaces: nightly CI artifacts, PR comment hidden metadata, Slack message metadata. PR comment staleness detection integrated with 👍-to-approve workflow (extract version from comment metadata, compare against tool, regenerate if stale). Schema changelog exposed via `--schema-changelog --from=1 --to=2` flag. Generalizable pattern identified: any CLI tool embedding commands in structured output needs schema versioning (fork-string, rename-surface, Playwright audit). Comparable: REST API versioning (multiple versions simultaneously vs single-version here), Docker tags/digests (mutable/immutable versioning), Terraform state file dual-version pattern (exact precedent: format version integer + software version semver), Protocol Buffers wire format version. Three player journeys: Aarav CI failure at 6:47 AM caught by pre-eval version check with clear "re-run audit" message (yellow not red — staleness not failure); Priya bumping schema version with co-located changelog in same file as the constant (can't forget); Dev learning tool_version vs repair_command_schema_version distinction via `--schema-version` and `--schema-changelog` flags, forming "cache ETag" mental model. Five new sub-aspects discovered: automated schema change detection (CI drift detector), version bump as team-affecting event (migration window), IDE extension schema version protocol, cross-tool schema version alignment (audit envelope meta-version), schema changelog as testable artifact. |
| 85 | 2026-03-14 | 2.00b — Simulated intelligence: deterministic systems designed to FEEL autonomous | 1 run | Six-layer personality system layered on top of fully deterministic engine (2.00a): Layer 1 callsign generator (themed names per unit type — bird names for scouts, blade names for strikers, weather names for relays, title names for command); Layer 2 idle micro-animations seeded from buffer state (empty=searching, full=shimmer, threat=lean, waiting=cyan glow, received=antenna flash, fired=expanding circles); Layer 3 personality-flavored rule templates (Cautious/Aggressive/Paranoid/Social as named starting points, fully editable); Layer 4 signal format quirks (via: callsign + confidence: label in buffer entries — anthropomorphizes data provenance); Layer 5 personality death animations (cautious=slow collapse sensor dimming last, aggressive=explosive burst, paranoid=staccato signal burst, social=antenna reaching toward teammate); Layer 6 near-miss rendering (micro-hesitation toward almost-triggered rule's target, creating illusion of deliberation). Core thesis: intelligence is a rendering problem — the agent doesn't need to BE smart, it needs to LOOK smart, via legible cause-and-effect, varied behavior, personality consistency, micro-telegraphing, and social legibility. Zero mechanical cost: nothing changes the deterministic engine. Five strengths: diagnostic anthropomorphism (panic animation = legible buffer-full state), named units create narrative stakes, streaming/community content generation, mental model isomorphism (personality vocab maps 1:1 to mechanical reality), zero engine cost. Five weaknesses: anthropomorphism trap (players blame agents instead of their own designs), naming attachment discourages redesign, templates as crutches suppressing custom rule building, near-miss rendering confuses beginners, visual noise at 8+ agents. Comparable games: Pac-Man ghosts (minimal presentation creates lasting character attachment), Dwarf Fortress (500+ traits but only mechanically-reflected ones generate narrative), RimWorld (consistency > variety for character feel), XCOM nicknames (names are cheapest personality layer), Into the Breach (contextual intelligence from board state not agent complexity), Batman Arkham (animation > decision logic). Four player journeys: Tomás 16yo first encounter with named agents — "Osprey is the eyes, Bolo is the fist" (personality creates military metaphor without instruction); Adaeze 29yo UX designer using personality animations as diagnostic entry points — "Heron looks stressed" leads to buffer eviction policy fix; Riku 38yo SRE streamer narrating Arbiter's incident response — match report reads as production incident postmortem; Sofia 52yo math teacher watching deterministic hooks as soap opera character interactions — independently invents "feedback loop" concept from named agent wiring. | 84 | 2026-03-14 | 4.69e-viii — Tag expiry and automatic sunset | 1 run | Five design options for adversarial tag lifecycle management preventing stale tags from accumulating over long competitive careers. Core problem: tags are judgments made at a specific moment against a specific config in a specific competitive context — all three change over time, but the tag doesn't know. The Stale Tag Problem compounds via accumulation (15-20 tags over 10 seasons), invisible distortion (silent match exclusion from career analysis), false confidence (stale tag hiding current structural weakness), and cognitive archaeology (forgotten tag rationale). Option A: Season Timer — fixed 3-season TTL with renewal prompt showing original reason, current concentration, config changes since tagging, and with/without impact preview; grace period (14 days) before deactivation; graduated renewal (3 seasons vs. 1 season); weakness: fixed TTL wrong for everyone, renewal fatigue at 8+ tags. Option B: Soft Decay — caps widen 5% per season, binary tags convert to caps, per-cluster locks revert to auto; aging flag icon fades from solid red to ghost outline; self-correcting: if opponent still adversarial, decayed cap eventually lets enough matches through to re-trigger cluster detection; if opponent stopped, decay converges to irrelevance; strength: zero-maintenance correct outcomes; weakness: loss of calibrated precision, unpredictable diagnostic shifts. Option C: Activity-Based Sunset — tags expire when evidence weakens, not on calendar; three evidence signals (concentration drop below threshold for 2 analyses, config change on tagged agent, match absence for 2+ seasons); Tag Health dashboard with traffic-light evidence status (Active/Weakened/Expired); 2-of-3 signals at ⚠ triggers review, all 3 at ✗ triggers deactivation; strength: evidence-based tags that ARE valid never expire; weakness: complexity, config change noise, exploitable by sophisticated opponent who pauses targeting. Option D: Hybrid Timer+Evidence+Decay (RECOMMENDED) — three-layer system: Layer 1 evidence monitoring (continuous, passive), Layer 2 soft decay (3% per season, slower than Option B), Layer 3 hard 6-season ceiling (absolute maximum, forces re-creation from scratch with current evidence); layers interact: evidence triggers early review during decay phase, decay softens impact before ceiling hits, ceiling guarantees no infinite accumulation; batch review UI for multiple simultaneous ceiling hits with system recommendations (remove if concentration below threshold, re-tag at recalibrated value if still high) and "Apply all recommendations" one-click button; re-tagging cost is low (30 seconds with pre-filled current data). Option E: Pinned Immortal — 2 never-expire slots (inspired by Overwatch's 3 pinned avoid slots); drag-to-pin with parallax and magnetic snap animation; pinned tags get annual context-check prompt (informational, cannot deactivate); deep crimson ⚑ with gold border visual distinction; pin constraint forces prioritization of which adversaries are truly permanent. RECOMMENDED: D+E composite — "The Full Lifecycle" combining hybrid expiry with 2 pinned slots. Aging flag visual: solid red (season 0-1) → dusty rose (2-3) → pale pink (4-5) → ghost outline flickering at 0.33Hz (season 6 ceiling). Storage model includes created_context (original reason, config snapshot, pool size, APS at creation) and renewal_history. Interaction effects: with per-opponent cap (UI shows "set value: 50%, effective after decay: 59%" with ghost marker on slider), with APS (post-removal APS delta surfaced in next analysis as re-tag prompt), with pool calibration (pool context stored at creation enables cross-season comparison), with per-cluster locks (suppress locks decay faster than include locks — 3 vs. 4 seasons — because suppression is more dangerous when wrong), with community signal (expired tags excluded from aggregate count), with compound detection (coalition expiry checks compound score not individual concentrations), with counterfactual history (expired tags preserved as historical data), with redesign mode (pre-redesign tag review reminder). Comparable systems: Overwatch Avoid as Teammate (7-day expiry + 3 pins, community frustration with limit — lesson: pin constraint will feel restrictive, compensate with quality of timed experience), software feature flag TTL (LaunchDarkly mandatory expiry, Uber Piranha automated cleanup of 2000 stale flags — lesson: manual cleanup doesn't scale, batch tools essential), DNS TTL (per-record freshness vs. query cost trade-off), passport expiry (expired ≠ deleted, streamlined renewal vs. from-scratch), Spotify artist block (silent behavior divergence when block outlives context). Four player journeys: Priya (30, software engineer — batch reviews 3 ceiling tags; removes VoidPhase who she doesn't remember and NightOwl whose 9-season-old binary tag has decayed to irrelevant 44% cap; re-tags CrimsonByte at tighter 48% cap; discovers NightOwl's stale tag was hiding a real SCOUT-B structural weakness for years — "Your oldest tag was hiding your biggest problem"), Marcus (34, Architect streamer — re-tags PhantomEdge for 3rd time, discovers pinned slots via tooltip, drags to pin with magnetic snap animation, gold-bordered pin icon becomes streaming content, chat discusses pin allocation strategy, "tag management is content" becomes community meme; annual context-check prompt takes 5 seconds), Tomás (17, Silver first season — evidence weakening notification on Season 2 binary tag; side-by-side with/without preview shows almost no difference; removes tag; learns vocabulary shift from "tags as moral judgments" to "tags as calibration instruments"; learning tooltip reinforces temporal nature), Keiko (42, accessibility-focused — screen reader structured review with ARIA landmarks, explicit "Tag age: N seasons of 6" replacing visual flag color, keyboard navigation R/arrow/Tab/Enter/P, accepts both recommendations in 30 seconds, skips pin decision). Sensory: aging flag 6-step color fade from RGB(220,50,50) to RGB(200,170,170,0.4); decay notification descending minor-scale three-note tone (each softer); ceiling notification clean neutral chime + brief silence; batch review cascade 100ms staggered slide-in with green/blue recommendation washes; drag-to-pin 3px Y-offset parallax + amber glow + 📌 360° spin + magnetic metallic snap; pinned tag 18px deep crimson with 1px gold border at permanent full mast. TikTok clip: scroll through 8 tags at various fade levels, hover ghost flag ("Season 1, Analysis #1 — your first tag"), tap Remove, particles dissolve, career analysis shifts, hidden cluster appears, eyes widen — "Your oldest tag was hiding your biggest problem." 5 new aspects discovered (tag renewal as competitive signal, batch review UX for 15+ tags, tag inheritance across season resets, deactivated tag reactivation flow, tag expiry as config hygiene tutorial). |
| 83 | 2026-03-14 | 4.69e-vii — Per-cluster adversarial exclusion | 1 run | Four design options for surgical per-cluster adversarial tagging where a player excludes an opponent from specific agent clusters but not others. | 82 | 2026-03-14 | 4.69e-vi — Concentration threshold calibration for dense opponent pools | 1 run | Four design options for adapting adversarial detection thresholds to small competitive brackets where normal match variance mimics adversarial concentration. Core problem: in a 12-player pool, expected per-opponent concentration is 9.1%, making fixed 50% cap thresholds miss real adversaries in large pools and false-positive in small ones. Option A: Contextual Denominator — adaptive_threshold = base_multiplier × (1/N) + floor_value; 3× expected as default (37.5% for 8-player, 15% for 20-player, 5% floor for 100+); expected frequency marker (▲) on cap slider with gradient ribbon showing excess; auto-calibrate button for one-click sensible defaults; info badge for small brackets (<30 players). Option B: Bayesian Surprise Score — surprise = -log₂(P(concentration ≥ C \| match_freq = F, cluster_size = K)); binomial proportion test per-opponent per-cluster; scores in bits (>4 bits = flagged); composable for compound detection (sum individual surprises); teaches expected-vs-observed reasoning that parallels buffer health assessment; weakness: "bits" vocabulary unfamiliar to casual players. Option C: Graduated Threshold Table — lookup table of defaults by pool size tier (5-8, 9-15, 16-30, 31-60, 61-100, 100+); bracket size update prompt at season start with Auto-adjust/Keep/Customize; simplest to understand but crudest granularity; doesn't handle non-uniform matchmaking. Option D: Effective Pool Size — inverse Herfindahl-Hirschman Index (1/Σfᵢ²) computes matchmaker-corrected N; 50-player bracket with aggressive SBMM → effective N of 7.8; sparkline hover chart showing descending opponent frequency staircase with effective-N cutoff line; handles SBMM distortion correctly but volatile early-season and harder to explain. Key insight: pool correction can INCREASE APS (effective pool smaller than raw N means same adversarial density is more concentrated). Interaction effects: cap slider storage (absolute vs. relative-to-expected), APS inflation (directly solves 4.69e-v-c), compound detection gate calibration, tag validity across pool changes, campaign mode should disable pool calibration entirely. Comparables: chess.com arena "Avoid Player" (binary, doesn't scale), Dota 2/LoL high-MMR small pools (accepted variance, no correction), VALORANT Radiant encounter tracking (wider matchmaking windows as alternative). Three player journeys: Priya (28, Diamond, 11-player bracket — sets pool-calibrated cap on CloudNine at 35%, discovers pool-corrected APS drops from 0.14 to 0.06 "Clean," realizes VortexBlue tag may have been false positive), Tomás (19, first ranked season, 8-player bracket — small-pool advisory prevents premature tagging on SolarWind at 80% concentration but 4.8 bits surprise; learns statistical reasoning through the "Based on 5 matches" confidence note), Wei (35, GM, 200-player bracket — effective pool 28.4 via SBMM; discovers PhantomEdge at 28% concentration is 8× expected in effective pool; compound coalition detection finds KaijuStrike+PhantomEdge at 11.2 bits combined).
